         Dear Sir :                                                                                                •
                                           Re : U. F. O. Sighting
                                                Near Town of Souris ,        .'
                                                Souris , Manitoba - 12 APR 78 .
                                  At 8 :40 P.M,. Central Standard Time , 12 "APR 78 ,
         Andrew William WATAl-iANUK (BD : 13 AUG 40} , Farmer, residi ng three miles
         west , attended at the R. C. M,. POLICE detac~ent i~ Souris to report what
         he t hought was a "U. F.o, •I hovering in the sky west of his farm and south ,
         of P.T. Hwy. ,?2. WATM·tlNUK stated he sighted a similar object about the
I'
     •   same t ime on Sunday, 9 APR 78 . He described the object as being approx-
         imately J 000 ft . off the groun4, three dimensional spherical centre
         coloured bright yellow, changing to bright red in the sp~ere • s extremities .
         Two "wing- tUke" extensions opposite each other on t he sphere (horizontally}
         cast a pulsating bright bluish green light with a periodic red flash . ffe ,
         stated the movement of this object was vert ical, directly over a neigh-
         boUr ' s (Keith Orville KOMTZIE, born 11 MAR 50} farm . The object then
         moved ltorizbntally west , approximately one mile and hovered over the ·     ..
         f~ of Paul David ADDISON, born 7 FEB 4J ,~ere it ' s vertical movement
         continued. The object then made a quick c1~ar1 movement over the                1
         ADDISON farm and disappear~d , re- appearing approximately five minutes
         later over the KONTZIE farm . \~ATAMANUK stated the object then moved
         very slowly w~stward without a sou.nd, finally disappearing . He stated
         the sky was clear at the ti~e and he kept thi s object in sight for app-
         roximately tw9 and one- half hours .
               \     '           At 9:10P. M., 12 APR 78 , t wo members attended
         with WATAMANUK at his farm , · later joined by the N. O.O. In Charge .
         WATAMANUK pointed out the object and with the naked eye we 5ould see

                  On Monday, 76 May 15, «P approximately 0310 hours , I woke up
          and decided to go 4ownstairs for a .glass of milk.

          glass of milk when I' heard a very loud high pitched sound. It was simUF .
          in sound to a noise consistant ·to that of a jet aircraft.
                     The sky at this time was clear. I then walked over t o the
          living r oom window to take a iook outside to determine where the noise
          was coming from . I noticed an object in the sky towards the West; It
          was approximatel y 100 feet in the ai.r . The ob~ect was flat and sbape4
          somewhat like a fish , and was approximately 150' long. The object
          rotated onto its side and stayed there for a period of time. When' the
          object became stationary the high pitched sound ceased. I held observation
        ' on it for approximately· li to 2 minutes. The object then departed at a
          high rate of speed and quickly disappeared. The underneath portion of the
          object looked similar to this diagram and was black in colo~.

                             ..                                                     .           TMDUCTION • •
                                                                                                                      •
                                                                                                     ..
                        I was getting oot of ''Motel du VillageH at 1:30 on   I
                  26 June 78. 1 saw something which I thought was the moon .
                  It was red. 1 headed for Caraquet. As I was driving, ft
                  became rounder and rounder and it got closer and closer to me.
                  When I left the Motel, it was sort of beside me and at so•e
                  distance. later, it was facing me. It wasn't ever stable.
                  It could go up and down. When I arrived home, 1 turned fn the
                  driveway and the object was at the neighbour's, in the trees.
                  When 1 got out of the car. the object left rapidly towards the
                  bay. I arrived home at 1:45.                          •

              •        Can you describe
                                •
                                        the object?                                 lts shape?

                       (Drawing of the object in orfgf nal tex.t.)
                                                                                                          •
                       The right-hand side wis very round, the left-hand side
                  was more elongated. It was a reddish oralge. It was lu~inous
                  1 n itself.

          be:t.ng the complainant , Bonnie McCALLIJ1.1 of t he Laybolt Road area .
          She stated that hers elf along with her son , Robert \'lade McCALLUM ,
          age "10 1 and a Brenda FISHER age .17 , had seen this U. F.O. Bonnie ·
          McCALLUM is thirty years old . ·They all stated that they had seen thi~
          big light hovering over the trees to the west . They described i t as
          haYing one big light in the center and colored Ughts strung across
          the bottom of t~ object~ It was described as being half-moon
          shaped , approximately fifteen feet in diameter , with one large whit'e
          light in the center, and amaller colored lights on each aide .
          FISHER stated that it had a yellow and orahge light on the left
    •
          side and a fe d and blue light on the right side. They st~f-ed that
        · while they watched this object, in hovered over tbe trees on the
          horizon , approximateLy one mile off to the 'lest of Laybolt Road .
          They stated tbt ~ter hovering for approximately one-half hour , ~t
          sunk down out of sight behind the trees for a couple of minutes
          then just took off towards the easterly direction . They said they
          were at their home on the Laybolt Road .when this sighting occurred ,
          and that the sighting lasted approximately thr ee quarters of an hour.
          McOALLUM~stated that she watched it for about half an hour before
•         s he fin~ly called in the complaint . They all stated that •this
          object didn ' t make any noise and just hovered over the trees .

         lived next door , also saw this object . Upon questioning STONE, I
         was advised by her that this object was an ,airplane , or eo abe

                            Woodrow ELLSWORTH
                            Report of Unidentified Flying Object
                            Piccadilly, Nfld. - 78 AUG 31

           •                This is further to telex message
               1.          On 78 AUG 31, Woodrow ELLSWORTH (B: 52 PEB 04) of l Stanley
               Place, Stephenville, Nfld., came to the R.C.M. Police Detachment at
               Stephenville, Nfld. and reported that at 2145 hrs on t~e above date, be
               ~as travelling east on Highway 463 in Piccadilly, Nfld . when he and a
               paasenge~ in his car noticed a large bright object in the sky moving
               from west to east. ELLSWORTH was a taxi driver for a local taxi
               c~pany in Stephenville, Nfld. and was in the Picc~dilly area when he
               picked up Clancy WHITE hitch hiking on the road.
               2.           ELLSWORTH reports that at the time of the sightin$ the sky
               was plear •and a~ar lit, with the northern liJhts • slightly vis1ble.
                                                                   '
               3.          When the object was sighted, ELLSWORTH was approximately
               one mile east of Piccad1lly Beach.         '                     1
           '
             4.          There were no reported bursts of apeed.       The object moved
           - slowly and steadily.                       ••
               5.           The object glowed brightly. It .appeared dull as compared
               to the moon and the brightness of the object hurt the eyes when     .
               ELLSWORTH' looked directly at it. He compared the brightness of the
    \          object to the brightness of an arc welding flash.                  ·
               6.           The object did not look like •a shooting star. On.e witneu
               repot:ted the object as very bright red 1on the inside and bright blue on
               the outside, with no tail at the rear .     •     ,
               7.          The object was described as aomewha~ tubular in shape and
               streamlined like a commercial aircraft.
•              8.           The object was · observed for approximately five ,to six
               minutes.
               9.           Both witneeaes report no sound coming from the object .
               10.         ELLSWORTH reports the object as being approxiaately 100
               above the ground, while WHITE states the object was very distant in
               the sky, so far away in fact that he could not comment on .it• size.
               11.         ELLSWORTH states that two or three red lifhta were fla•h
               on the inside of the object and that th~ object had w1ndowa siailar
               thft found in co.mercial aircraft.

      REMARKS:
                                                                                                   ..
                  Tbe - sishting of this object was related to the writer by
      Woodrow ELLSWORTH 1n the first instance, with WHITE being unconcerned
      whether or not the matter was reported. Since the time of the sighting,
      efforts were made to l ocate WHITE, however, during the summer he had no
      fixed address and at the time ELLSWORTH did not know t~e name of the '
      person he had picked up.
                  WHITE's account of what ·happened is verr different from
      that of ELLSWORTH. ·WHITE claims he recalls the inc dent v1vidly and
      that he could not give an adequate description of the object . ·
              This report forwarded direc't to the National Research
  Council, Planetory Soiences Section, 100 Sussex Drive, Ottawa, Ontario.

                          Dear Sir:
    •                  1.        On 78 Sept li at approximately 12:00 midnight , Mr •
                       Fred WESOLOWSKI , his mother Tina WESOLOWSKI and sister
                       Laverna WESOLOW6KI were proceeding south-east on highway #41                                     •
                       near Aberdeen , Sask . WESOLOWSKI reports that he observed the
                       f ollowing:                          '
                                 Four pulsating silver/blue lights in a vertical
                                 column at approximately 1 mil~ dis tance . The           1
                                 lights then separated and formed various patterns
                                 at tiroefJ and at other times l'lere (lt random . Lights
                                 continued to pulsate rapidly and change color from
                                 silver/blue to yello~t to pink several t imes in no
                                 sequence . Lights travelled very,fast horizontally .
                   • •           WESOLOWSKI feels there \''&8 a solid obj~ct at the
                                 source of each light b~t c6uld not make out any
                                 discernable shape.                           I

                          2.       WESOLOWSKI lt!ltched the lildlts for approximately 1
                          minute . It is not kno~m in what direction or manner the
                          lights left. WESOLOWSKI ~,a vis ibly shaken by the sighting
                          but in my opinion , as \>tell as t wo other members of this Det-
                          achment; he is a reliable witness and is mentally competent .
                          J,       Saskatoon Airport control reports no unus ual sight-
                          ings or trackings during time in q~~'tion .

          ..                          Chester LETHBRIDGE
                                      Report of U, F.O . Sighting
                                      Clarenville , Nfldo - 78- lQ-12 ·                                                 ..

                                       AT 0) 00, 78-lQ-12, Mr. & Mrs. Chester LETHBRIDGE
                         Marine Drive , Clarenville , Nfld. observed the following desc;ibed
                         object for approximately two hours. Mr. LETHBRIDGE observed it
                         with the aid of binoculars from outside his residence . The sky was
                         clear at this time and the object was obseryed over Random Island ,
                         Trinity Bay Nfld. Object described as circular, 35 - 40 feet in
                         diameter, dashing red, blue arul yellow lightis . It was approximately
                         500 feet above sea leVel. and there was no mov 1emept . Mrs.• LE1HBRIDGE
                         reported this to the Department of National Defence , Gander , Nfld.
                         at t\le time.                      •
                         2.                          These witnesses are of unknown reliability.
                         ).           A telex has been forwarded to the National Research
                         Council , Ottawa.

                                                                                                          I
                Brian CHOBOTAR, age 14 years, 1199 Grandin Village, St . Albert,
        Alberta . Phone 458-0470, reported sighting on two occasions as unusual
        object in southwest sky and from two different locations between the
        hqurs of 2150 and 2215 MDT. At the time of the observation the sky was
        clear. The observer was situated on the first occasio~ at the corner
        of Greenbrier and Gatewood' Avenue in St. Albert, and on the .second
        occasion from his bedroom window. Other persons were with him
        and there were no similar report~ on the evening in question. Observer's
        ~&scription of . object is not ver~ concise and he tended to emphasize
        that it contained two white lights and one red but was unable to judge
      • size or shape . On each occasion sightings were about S - 7 minutes
        duration . Supon receipt of the sighting reports, C.A.F. Base Opetations
        Officer, Captain TYMCHUK, was contacted to ascertain if he had any similar
        reports .. He advised he had not.
               Captain TYMCHUK of C.A.P . Base Operations Officer reports that
        he has triangulated the area and only normal flight traffic was reported.
        A number ~f persons tn the area kave been contacted and have not reported
        anything unusual. A number of people have seen lights however after
        watching for a while determined they were definitely ~~rcraft.
                   The only witnesses reliability is felt ~o be dubious:
               This · office feels that the reported sighting is unfounded, and
        this file is to be concluded.. ,. I, ('\ ~ ? ·f•J I•
